    \mathcal{S} &\leq \sum_{T \in \T(\bar{\kappa})}\big(\mathcal{S}(T)-1\big).
\end{align}
For each $T=T^M$, we further split $\mathcal{S}(T)$ into sums over subsets of models $M$ that have $u_j$ more parameters than $T^M$ in block $j$, and are missing $w_j$ parameters from $T^M$ in block $j$.
Specifically, consider models $M$ such that, for all $j$, $|M_j\setminus T^M_j|=u_j$ and $|T^M_j\setminus M_j|=w_j$ with $u_j \in \{0,\ldots,p_j-s_j,\ldots,p_j-s_j+|S_j^{S}(\bar{\kappa})|\}$ and $w_j \in \{0,\ldots,|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})|\}$. Denote by
$$S^{\bu}_{\bw}(T)=\sum_{M \in \M(T) \cup T:\forall \,j |M_j\setminus S_j|=u_j, |S_j\setminus M_j|=w_j } e^{- \sum_{j=1}^b u_j\big(\ln(p_j-s_j)+\delta \tfrac{f_j}{2}\big)-\sum_{j=1}^b w_j\big(\ln(s_j)+\delta \tfrac{e'_j}{2}\big)}.$$
We get
\begin{equation}\label{eq:uygfew2prob}
    \mathcal{S}(T)=\sum_{w_1=0}^{|S^L_1(\bar{\kappa})|}\cdots\sum_{w_b=0}^{|S^L_b(\bar{\kappa})|}\sum_{u_1=0}^{p_1-s_1+|S_1^S|}\cdots\sum_{u_b=0}^{p_b-s_b+|S_b^S|}S^{\bu}_{\bw}(T).
\end{equation}
The number of models missing, for all $j$, $w_j$ out of the $|S^L_j(\bar{\kappa})|$ large active parameters and having $u_j$ inactive or small active parameters from $B_j$ is $\prod_{j=1}^b {\binom{p_j-s_j+|S^S_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{u_j}}{\binom{|S^L_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{w_j}}$. We thus have
\begin{align*}
    S^{\bu}_{\bw}(T)&= \bigg(\prod_{j=1}^b {\binom{p_j-s_j+|S^S_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{u_j}}{\binom{|S^L_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{w_j}}\bigg)e^{- \sum_{j=1}^b u_j\big(\ln(p_j-s_j)+\delta \tfrac{f_j}{2}\big)-\sum_{j=1}^b w_j\big(\ln(s_j)+\delta \tfrac{e'_j}{2}\big)} \\
    &= \prod_{j=1}^b {\binom{p_j-s_j+|S^S_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{u_j}}e^{- u_j\big(\ln(p_j-s_j)+\delta \tfrac{f_j}{2}\big)}{\binom{|S^L_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{w_j}}e^{-w_j\big(\ln(s_j)+\delta \tfrac{e'_j}{2}\big)}.
\end{align*}
Inputting the expression above in \eqref{eq:uygfew2prob} and factorizing over terms in $u_j$ and $w_j$ gives
\begin{align*}
    \mathcal{S}(T)\leq\prod_{j=1}^b & \Bigg(\sum_{u_j=0}^{p_j-s_j+|S^S_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{\binom{p_j-s_j+|S^S_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{u_j}}e^{- u_j(\ln(p_j-s_j)+\delta \tfrac{f_j}{2})}\Bigg)\\&.\Bigg(
    \sum_{w_j=0}^{|S^L_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{\binom{|S^L_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{w_j}}
    e^{-w_j(\ln(s_j)+\delta \tfrac{e'_j}{2})}\Bigg).
\end{align*}
By the bound in \eqref{eq:upperboudbinom} and taking the terms in $u_j=0$ and $w_j=0$ out of the sums above, %taking the term in 0 out of the sum above, 
we have
\begin{align}\label{eq:ogrprob}
    \mathcal{S}(T)\leq\prod_{j=1}^b & \left(1+\sum_{u_j=1}^{p_j-s_j+|S_j^{S}(\bar{\kappa})|}e^{-u_j\left(\delta \tfrac{f_j}{2}-\ln\big(1+\frac{|S^{S}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j-s_j}\big)-1\right)}\right)\\&.\left(1+
    \sum_{w_j=1}^{|S_j^{L}(\bar{\kappa})|}    e^{-w_j\left(\delta \tfrac{e'_j}{2}+\ln\big(\frac{s_j}{|S_j^{L}(\bar{\kappa})|}\big)-1\right)}\right).\nonumber
\end{align}
Denote
$$
d_j\;=\;e^{1+\ln\big(1+\frac{|S^{S}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j-s_j}\big)-\delta \tfrac{f_j}{2}},\qquad h_j\;=\;e^{1-\ln\big(\frac{s_j}{|S_j^{L}(\bar{\kappa})|}\big)-\delta \tfrac{e'_j}{2}}.
$$
By assumption $|S^{S}_j(\bar{\kappa})|=O(p_j-s_j)$, and by definition of $|S_j^{L}(\bar{\kappa})|$ we have $s_j\geq|S_j^{L}(\bar{\kappa})|$. By Assumptions~\hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter} and the definition of $|S_j^{L}(\bar{\kappa})$, we also have $f_j\to\infty$ and $e'_j\to\infty$, and then $\lim_{n \to \infty}= d_j= \lim_{n \to \infty} h_j=0$. Using the properties of geometric series, 
 for every $j$ we have
\begin{eqnarray*}
    &1+\sum_{u_j=1}^{p_j-s_j+|S_j^{S}(\bar{\kappa})|}e^{-u_j\left(\delta \tfrac{f_j}{2}-\ln\big(1+\frac{|S^{S}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j-s_j}\big)-1\right)}
    =\frac{1-d_j^{p_j-s_j+|S_j^{S}(\bar{\kappa})|+1}}{1-d_j} \\
    &1+\sum_{w_j=1}^{|S_j^{L}(\bar{\kappa})|}    e^{-w_j\left(\delta \tfrac{e'_j}{2}+\ln\big(\frac{s_j}{|S_j^{L}(\bar{\kappa})|}\big)-1\right)} =\frac{1-h_j^{|S_j^{L}(\bar{\kappa})|+1}}{1-h_j},
\end{eqnarray*}
where both expressions converge to $1$ as $n$ grows. By \eqref{eq:gepihwprob2prob} and \eqref{eq:ogrprob}:
$$\mathcal{S}\leq\sum_{T\in\T}\Bigg(\prod_{j=1}^b\Big(\frac{1-d_j^{p_j-s_j+|S_j^{S}(\bar{\kappa})|+1}}{1-d_j}\Big)\Big(\frac{1-h_j^{|S_j^{L}(\bar{\kappa})|+1}}{1-h_j}\Big)-1\Bigg).$$
Each of the summand vanishes as $n\to\infty$. Moreover, by assumption $|S^I(\bar{\kappa})|=O(1)$ and then $|\T(\bar{\kappa})|=2^{|S^I(\bar{\kappa})|}=O(1)$. We thus have $\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathcal{S}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{M \in \M\setminus \T(\bar{\kappa})} \E\left(p(M\mid \by, \btheta)\right)=0$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Convergence of the expected sum of posterior inclusion probabilities}
Lemma~\ref{prop:shatconsistenceprob} is analogous to Proposition~\ref{prop:shatconsistence} and shows asymptotic bounds on the expected sum of posterior inclusion probabilities, under no assumption on signal strength other minimal technical requirements. It is used in the proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:empbayesselconsistuprob}.
\begin{lem}
    \label{prop:shatconsistenceprob}
Assume \hyperlink{lab:A1}{A1} and \hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter}, $|S^{I}(\bar{\kappa})|=O(1)$ and $|S^{S}_j(\bar{\kappa})|=O(p_j-s_j)$ for every $j=1,\ldots,b$. Then
$$ \,\, \frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}\leq \lim_{n\to\infty}E\bigg(\frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j} p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta)\bigg)\leq \frac{s_j-|S^{S}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}\qquad\text{for all }j=1,\ldots,b.$$
\end{lem}

\begin{proof}
Denote
$$A_j:=\frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})|i \in  M} p(M \mid \by, \btheta) \quad\text{and}\quad  C_j:=\frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j}\sum_{M \not\in \T(\bar{\kappa})|i \in  M} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)$$
where $\T(\bar{\kappa})$ is defined in \eqref{eq:Tkappaprob}.
For every $j=1,\ldots,b$, we have the decomposition
\begin{gather}\label{eq:decompshatprob}
    \frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j} p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta)
    =\frac{\sum_{i:z_i=j} \sum_{M  | i \in M}  p(M \mid \by, \btheta)}{p_j}
   =A_j\,+\, C_j.
\end{gather}
To show the lower bound, we decompose $A_j$
\begin{eqnarray}
  A_j
   &=& \sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)\sum_{i:z_i=j}\frac{I(i\in  M)}{p_j}\nonumber\\
   &=& \sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)\sum_{i:z_i=j, i \in S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})}\frac{I(i\in  M)}{p_j}+\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)\sum_{i:z_i=j, i\not\in S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})}\frac{I(i\in  M)}{p_j}\nonumber\\
   &=& \frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)+\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)\sum_{i:z_i=j, i\not\in S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})}\frac{I(i\in  M)}{p_j}\label{eq:wigrprob}.
\end{eqnarray}
where the last equality follows from $I(i\in  M)=1$ for all $i \in S^L(\bar{\kappa})$ when $M\in\T(\bar{\kappa})$. The rightmost term above and $C_j$ are nonnegative, then by the linearity of the expectation
$$E\bigg(\frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j} p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta)\bigg)\geq\frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})}E( p(M \mid \by, \btheta)).$$
By Lemma~\ref{thm:convtoTprob}, $\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})}\E( p(M \mid \by, \btheta))=1$. It follows that $$\lim_{n\to\infty}E\bigg(\frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j} p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta)\bigg)\geq \frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}\quad\text{ for every   }j=1,\ldots,b.$$

We now prove the upper bound. Recall that $\T(\bar{\kappa})$ by definition includes models that have no small signals, i.e. all parameters are in $S^L(\bar{\kappa}) \cup S^I(\bar{\kappa})$. That is,
%Since 
for all $M\in\T(\bar{\kappa})$, we have that $I(i\in  M)=0$ for all $i \in\{i:z_i=j, i\not\in(S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})\cup S^{I}_j(\bar{\kappa}))\}$. Hence, $A_j$ in \eqref{eq:wigrprob} satisfies
\begin{align*}
    A_j
   &= \frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)+\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)\sum_{i \in S^{I}_j(\bar{\kappa})}\frac{I(i\in  M)}{p_j}\\
   &\leq\frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)+ \frac{|S^{I}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)
\end{align*}
where the inequality follows from $\sum_{i \in S^{I}_j(\bar{\kappa})} I(i\in  M) \leq |S_j^{I}(\bar{\kappa})|$  for all $M$. By \eqref{eq:decompshatprob}, we then have
\begin{equation}\label{eq:uuuuuprob}
    \frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j} p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta)\leq \frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})|+|S^{I}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta) + C_j.
\end{equation}
Moreover, for every $j=1,\ldots,b$, $C_j$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:uuuuuprob2}
    C_j = \sum_{M \not\in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)\sum_{i:z_i=j}\frac{I(i\in  M)}{p_j}\leq \sum_{M \not\in \T(\bar{\kappa})} p(M \mid \by, \btheta)
\end{equation}
where the inequality follows from $\sum_{i:z_i=j}\frac{I(i\in  M)}{p_j}\leq 1$ for all $M$.
Taking expectations in \eqref{eq:uuuuuprob} and \eqref{eq:uuuuuprob2} gives
\begin{equation*}
    E\bigg(\frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j} p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta)\bigg) 
    \leq \frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})|+|S^{I}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})}\E( p(M \mid \by, \btheta)) + \sum_{M \not\in \T(\bar{\kappa})}\E( p(M \mid \by, \btheta)).
\end{equation*}
 By Lemma~\ref{thm:convtoTprob}, we have on one hand $\lim_{n\to \infty}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa})}\E( p(M \mid \by, \btheta))= 1$ and, on the other hand, $\lim_{n\to \infty}\sum_{M \not\in\T(\bar{\kappa})}\E( p(M \mid \by, \btheta))= 0$. It follows that $$ \lim_{n\to \infty}E\bigg(\frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j} p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta)\bigg) \leq  \frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa})|+|S^{I}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}= \frac{s_j-|S^{S}_j(\bar{\kappa})|}{p_j}$$ for every $j=1,\ldots,b$, which proves the upper bound.
\end{proof}



\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:suffcondlinearmodel}}
Theorem~\ref{thm:suffcondlinearmodel} is a particular case of Lemma \ref{thm:convtoTprob}. Observe that under assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A5ter}{A5ter}, $S^L(\bar{\kappa})=S$ and $S^S(\bar{\kappa})=S^I(\bar{\kappa})=\emptyset$. It follows that $\T(\bar{\kappa})=S$. Assumptions \hyperlink{lab:A1}{A1} and \hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter} are also assumed to hold and, trivially, $|S^{I}(\bar{\kappa})|=O(1)$ and $|S^{S}_j(\bar{\kappa})|=O(p_j-s_j)$ for every $=1,\ldots,b$, then by Lemma \ref{thm:convtoTprob}, $\lim_{n \to \infty} \sum_{M \neq S} \E\left(p(M\mid \by, \btheta)\right)= 0$.


\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:empbayesselconsistuprob}}
The proof strategy is to apply Theorem~\ref{thm:suffcondlinearmodel} that requires Assumptions \hyperlink{lab:A1}{A1}, \hyperlink{lab:A3}{A3}, \hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter} and \hyperlink{lab:A5ter}{A5ter}. Recall that Theorem~\ref{theo:empbayesselconsistuprob} makes Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A1}{A1}, \hyperlink{lab:A3}{A3}, hence it suffices to show that \hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter} and \hyperlink{lab:A5ter}{A5ter} hold.
We first derive a convenient decomposition of the empirical Bayes penalties $\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j$. The second step of the proof is to show that, with probability going to 1, these $\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j$ satisfy Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter}. The third step consists in showing that Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A8bis}{A8bis} implies Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A5ter}{A5ter} for $\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j$. The convergence of $E\left(p(S\mid \by, \btheta^{(1)})\right)$ then follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:suffcondlinearmodel}.

In Step 1, from the choice $\btheta^{(0)}$ and the ensuing penalty $\bar{\kappa}^{(0)}$, we have
\begin{align*}
     \frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})
    &\;=\; \frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j}\,\,\sum_{M}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})I(i\in M)\\
   &\;=\; \frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{M }p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})\sum_{i:z_i=j}I(i\in M) \\
   &\;=\;\frac{s_j}{p_j}p(S\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) + \sum_{M  | M \neq S}\frac{|M_j|}{p_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}).
\end{align*}
Using that $p(S\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})=1-\sum_{M  | M \neq S}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})$, we get
\begin{equation*}
   \frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})
   =\frac{s_j}{p_j} + \sum_{M  | M \neq S}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{p_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}).
\end{equation*}
Consider the decomposition of the sum in the right-hand side above between the sum over models $M$ that contain more parameters than $S$ in block $b$ and the sum over those that contain less parameters than $S$ in block $b$. Denote
$$
    \OO_j^{(0)} := \sum_{M  | |M_j| > s_j}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{p_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})\quad\text{and}\quad
    U_j^{(0)} := \sum_{M  | |M_j| < s_j}\frac{s_j-|M_j|}{p_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}).
$$
%are overfitting in $B_j$ in the sense $|M_j|> s_j$ and those that are underfitting in $B_j$, in the sense $|M_j|<s_j$, we can write:
We have
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rewriteshatprob}
  \frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})  = \frac{s_j}{p_j}\,\,+\,\, \OO_j^{(0)}-\,\,U_j^{(0)}.
\end{equation}
Observe that we have the following decomposition of Step 2 penalties
\begin{align*}
    \bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j  =
    &\log(p_j-s_j) + \log\Big(\frac{\sqrt{1+gn}}{s_j}\Big) + \log\Bigg(\frac{p_j-\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) }{p_j-s_j}\Bigg) \\
    &+ \log\Bigg(\frac{s_j}{\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) }\Bigg).
\end{align*}
By \eqref{eq:rewriteshatprob}, it follows that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:decompstep2penprob}
\begin{aligned}
    \bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j = 
    &\log(p_j-s_j)+\log\Big(\frac{\sqrt{1+gn}}{s_j}\Big)+\log\Bigg(1-\frac{p_j(O^{(0)}_j-U^{(0)}_j)}{p_j-s_j}\Bigg)\\
    &+\log\Bigg(\frac{s_j}{\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) }\Bigg),
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
completing the first step of the proof.

We continue with the second step of the proof: showing that the $\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j$'s satisfy Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter} with probability going to 1. Recall that Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter} states that there exists $f_j\to \infty$ (as $n\to \infty$) such that for every sufficiently large $n$,
\begin{equation*}
    \bar{\kappa}_j \;=\;\log(p_j-s_j) + f_j.
\end{equation*}
Since $U_j^{(0)}$ is nonnegative, a lower bound on $\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j$ is %for every $b=1,\ldots,b$ is:
\begin{equation*}\label{eq:decompstep2pen2prob}
    \bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j
    \geq  \log(p_j-s_j)+\log\Big(\frac{\sqrt{1+gn}}{s_j}\Big)+\log\Bigg(1-\frac{p_j O^{(0)}_j}{p_j-s_j}\Bigg)+\log\Bigg(\frac{s_j}{\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) }\Bigg).
\end{equation*}
Plugging in the definition of $O^{(0)}_j$, we have that
$$\frac{p_j O^{(0)}_j}{p_j-s_j} =  \sum_{M  | |M_j| > s_j}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{p_j-s_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})\leq \sum_{M  | |M_j| > s_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})$$
where the inequality follows from $(|M_j|-s_j)/(p_j-s_j)\leq 1$ for all $M$.
Note that if $M$ is such that $|M_j|>s_j$, then $M\not\in\T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})$ (this follows immediately from the definition of $\T(\bar{\kappa})$ in \eqref{eq:Tkappaprob})  and therefore $\sum_{M  | |M_j| > s_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})\leq \sum_{M  \not \in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})$. Moreover, $\bar{\kappa}^{(0)}$ satisfies Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter} and the assumptions of Lemma~\ref{thm:convtoTprob} are met for $\bar{\kappa}^{(0)}$.
Then, by Lemma~\ref{thm:convtoTprob}, $\lim_{n\to\infty} \sum_{M \not\in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})}E(p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}))=\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{M  | |M_j| > s_j}E(p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})) = 0$. It follows that $\frac{p_j O^{(0)}_j}{p_j-s_j}$ vanishes in probability and so does $\log\Big(1-\frac{p_j O^{(0)}_j}{p_j-s_j}\Big)$. By Assumption~A4, we also have that $\log(\sqrt{1+gn}s_j^{-1})\to\infty$. 
Then, to show that Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter} holds with probability going to 1, it is enough to show that with probability going to 1, $\log(s_j/\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) )$ is nonnegative. Observe that all assumptions in Lemma~\ref{prop:shatconsistenceprob} are also met for $\bar{\kappa}^{(0)}$. By \eqref{eq:uuuuuprob} and \eqref{eq:uuuuuprob2} in the proof of Lemma~\ref{prop:shatconsistenceprob}, we have
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) }{p_j}
    \leq & \frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|+|S^{I}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) \\
    &+ \frac{1}{p_j} \sum_{M \not\in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}).
\end{align*}
By Lemma~\ref{thm:convtoTprob}, $\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})$ and $\sum_{M \not\in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})$ converge in probability to 1 and 0 respectively. We then have that, with probability going to 1,
\begin{equation*}
    \frac{\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) }{p_j}\leq \frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|+|S^{I}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|}{p_j},
\end{equation*}
which implies, with probability going to 1,
$$\log\Big(\frac{s_j}{\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) }\Big)\geq \log\bigg(\frac{s_j}{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|+|S^{I}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|} \bigg) \geq 0.$$
We then obtain that, with probability going to 1,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:eeeeeeehprob}
    \bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j
    \geq  \log(p_j-s_j)+\log\Big(\frac{\sqrt{1+gn}}{s_j}\Big)
\end{equation}
and that the $\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j$ satisfy Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter}, completing the second part of the proof.\medskip

For the third and final part of the proof, 
we now show that Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A8bis}{A8bis} implies Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A5ter}{A5ter} for the $\bar{\kappa}_j^{(1)}$ with probability going to 1. Recall that Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A5ter}{A5ter} for the $\bar{\kappa}_j^{(1)}$ states that for each block $j$ there exists $e_j\to \infty$ such that for large enough $n$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \sqrt{\frac{(1-\omega) n \rho(\bX)}{6}}{{\beta_{\min,j}^*}} \,- \,\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j} \;=\; \sqrt{\log(s_j)} + e_j .
    \end{equation*}
where $\omega$ takes value
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gammakebprob}
    \omega=\frac12\Big(1+\max_j\frac{\log(p_j-s_j)}{\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j}\Big).
\end{equation}
Observe that Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A8bis}{A8bis} and Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A5ter}{A5ter} take the same form. To show that Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A8bis}{A8bis} implies Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A5ter}{A5ter} for the $\bar{\kappa}_j^{(1)}$ with probability going to 1, it suffices to show that the following two inequalities
\begin{align}
    \sqrt{\frac{ (1-\omega)n\rho(\bX)}{6}}{\beta_{\min,j}^*} \,&\geq \,  \sqrt{\frac{ (1-\omega')n\rho(\bX)}{6}}{\beta_{\min,j}^*}, \label{eq:oaebrprob} \\
    - \,\sqrt{\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j} \,&\geq \,-\sqrt{\log\bigg(\frac{p}{|S^{L}(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|}-1\bigg)+\frac12\log(1+gn)}\label{eq:oaebrprob2}
\end{align}
hold with probability going to 1 for $\omega$ as in \eqref{eq:gammakebprob} and $\omega'=\frac12\big(1+\max_j \frac{\log(p_j-s_j)}{\log(p_j/s_j-1)+0.5\log(1+gn)}\big)$ (defined in Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A8bis}{A8bis}). We first show \eqref{eq:oaebrprob} holds with probability going to 1 and then that \eqref{eq:oaebrprob2} does too.

By \eqref{eq:eeeeeeehprob}, with probability going to 1,
$$ \frac{\log(p_j-s_j)}{\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j}
    \leq \frac{\log(p_j-s_j)}{\log(p_j-s_j)+\log\Big(\frac{\sqrt{1+gn}}{s_j}\Big)} =  \frac{\log(p_j-s_j)}{\log(p_j/s_j-1)+0.5\log(1+gn)} .$$
It follows that:
\begin{equation*}
    \omega=\frac12\Big(1+\max_{j=1, \ldots,b}\frac{\log(p_j-s_j)}{\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j}\Big) \leq \frac12\Big(1+\max_j \frac{\log(p_j-s_j)}{\log(p_j/s_j-1)+0.5\log(1+gn)}\Big)=\omega'
\end{equation*}
and \eqref{eq:oaebrprob} holds with probability going to 1.

We now upper bound $\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j$ to show \eqref{eq:oaebrprob2} holds with probability going to 1. Observe that 
\begin{align*}
    \log\Big(\frac{\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}) }{s_j}\Big)
    &= \log \left( 1 + \frac{\sum_{i:z_i=j}p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})  - s_j}{s_j}\right) \\
    &=\log\Big(1+\frac{p_j(O^{(0)}_j-U^{(0)}_j)}{s_j}\Big)
\end{align*}
where the second equality follows from \eqref{eq:rewriteshatprob}.
Plugging this expression into \eqref{eq:decompstep2penprob}, and using that $\OO_j^{(0)} \geq 0$, we have that  %Since $\OO_j^{(0)}$ is nonnegative, from \eqref{eq:decompstep2pen} we have that %, for every $b=1,\ldots,b$, an upper bound on $\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j$ is:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ggggggggprob}
    \bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j \leq \log\big(p_j-s_j\big)+\log\Big(\frac{\sqrt{1+gn}}{s_j}\Big)+\log\bigg(\frac{1+\frac{p_j}{p_j-s_j}U^{(0)}_j}{1-\frac{p_j}{s_j}U^{(0)}_j}\bigg).
\end{equation}
We split the sum in $U^{(0)}_j$ between models in $\T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})$ and those not in $\T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})$. 
$$U_j^{(0)} = \sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)}) | |M_j| < s_j}\frac{s_j-|M_j|}{p_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})+\sum_{M \not\in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)}) | |M_j| < s_j}\frac{s_j-|M_j|}{p_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)}).
$$
If $M\in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})$, then by definition $|M_j|\geq |S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|$ and thus $s_j-|M_j|\leq s_j-|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|$. A bound on $s_j-|M_j|$ for $M\not\in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})$ is simply $s_j-|M_j|\leq s_j$. It follows that
\begin{eqnarray*}
    U^{(0)}_j \leq \frac{s_j-|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)}) | |M_j| < s_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})+  \frac{s_j}{p_j}\sum_{M  \not\in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})| |M_j| < s_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})
\end{eqnarray*}
By Lemma~\ref{thm:convtoTprob}, $\sum_{M \in  \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})| |M_j| < s_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})$ and $\sum_{M  \not\in \T(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})| |M_j| < s_j}p(M\mid \by, \btheta^{(0)})$ converge in probability to 1 and 0 respectively. We then get that, with probability going to 1,
\begin{align}
    \frac{p_j}{p_j-s_j}U^{(0)}_j &\leq \frac{s_j-|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|}{p_j-s_j}\nonumber\\
    \frac{p_j}{s_j}U^{(0)}_j &\leq  \frac{s_j-|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|}{s_j}. \label{eq:gewouprob}
\end{align}
By the bounds above and \eqref{eq:ggggggggprob}, we have that with probability going to 1,
\begin{align}
  \bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j 
  &\leq \log\big(p_j-s_j\big)+\log\Big(\frac{\sqrt{1+gn}}{s_j}\Big)+\log\Bigg(\frac{1+\frac{s_j-|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|}{p_j-s_j}}{1- \frac{s_j-|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|}{s_j}}\Bigg) \nonumber\\
  &= \log\big(p_j-s_j\big) + \log\Big(\frac{\sqrt{1+gn}}{s_j}\Big) + \log\Bigg(\frac{\frac{p_j-|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|}{p_j-s_j}}{ \frac{|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|}{s_j}}\Bigg) \\
&=\log\big(p_j/|S^{L}_j(\bar{\kappa}^{(0)})|-1\big)+\frac{1}{2}\log(1+gn)\label{eq:finalupperboundkappajprob}
\end{align}
which shows \eqref{eq:oaebrprob2} holds with probability going to 1 and that
Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A8bis}{A8bis} implies Assumption~\hyperlink{lab:A5ter}{A5ter} holds for the $\bar{\kappa}^{(1)}_j$ with probability going to 1. 

Since Assumptions~\hyperlink{lab:A4ter}{A4ter} and \hyperlink{lab:A5ter}{A5ter} hold with probability going to 1, by Theorem~\ref{thm:suffcondlinearmodel}, $\lim_{n\to\infty}E \left[ p(S \mid \by, \hat{\theta}^{(1)}) \right]= 1$, as claimed.


\subsection{Proof of Theorem \ref{thm:ebayes_zerograd}}\label{proof:ebayes_zerograd}

Since $p(\by \mid \btheta)= \sum_{M} p(\by \mid M) p(M \mid \btheta)$
and $\nabla_{\btheta} \log p(M \mid \btheta)= [\nabla_{\btheta} p(M \mid \btheta)] / p(M \mid \btheta)$, we have that
\begin{align}
&\nabla_{\btheta}\log p(\by \mid \btheta)= \frac{\nabla_{\btheta} p(\by \mid \btheta)}{p(\by \mid \btheta)}= 
\frac{\sum_{M} p(\by \mid M) \nabla_{\btheta} p(M \mid \btheta)}{p(\by \mid \btheta)}= 
\nonumber \\
&= \frac{\sum_{M} p(\by \mid M) p(M \mid \btheta) \nabla_{\btheta} \log p(M \mid \btheta)}{p(\by \mid \btheta)}
= \sum_{M} p(M \mid \by, \btheta) \nabla_{\btheta} \log p(M \mid \btheta),
\nonumber
\end{align}
as claimed.

For $p(M \mid \btheta)=\prod_{i=1}^p \operatorname{Bern}\left(m_i ; \pi_i(\btheta)\right)$ for arbitrary differentiable $\pi_i()$, we get
\begin{align}
 \nabla_{\btheta} \log p(M \mid \btheta)=
\sum_{i=1}^p \nabla_{\btheta} \log p(m_i \mid \btheta)=
\sum_{i=1}^p \frac{\nabla_{\btheta} p(m_i \mid \btheta)}{p(m_i \mid \btheta)}.
\nonumber
\end{align}
%and hence
%\begin{align}
%\nabla_{\btheta}\log p(\by \mid \btheta)=
%E \left[ \sum_{i=1}^p \frac{\nabla_{\btheta} p(m_i \mid \btheta)}{p(m_i \mid \btheta)} \mid \by, \btheta \right].
%\nonumber
%\end{align}

Since $p(m_i \mid \btheta)= \pi_i(\btheta)^{m_i} [1 - \pi_i(\btheta)]^{1- m_i}$
\begin{align}
& \nabla_{\btheta} p(m_i \mid \theta)= \begin{cases}
\nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)= 
\frac{\nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)}{\pi_i(\btheta)} p(m_i \mid \btheta)
\mbox{, if } m_i=1
\\
- \nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)=
- \frac{\nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)}{1 - \pi_i(\btheta)} p(m_i \mid \btheta)
\mbox{, if } m_i=0
\end{cases}
\nonumber \\
&= p(m_i \mid \btheta) \nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta) \left[ \frac{m_i}{\pi_i(\btheta)} - \frac{1 - m_i}{1 - \pi_i(\btheta)} \right]
= p(m_i \mid \btheta) \frac{\nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)}{\pi_i(\btheta) [1 - \pi_i(\btheta)]} \left[ m_i - \pi_i(\btheta) \right].
\label{eq:grad_priorprob_indep}
\end{align}
This gives that
\begin{align}
&\nabla_{\btheta}\log p(\by \mid \btheta)= 
\E \left[  \sum_{i=1}^p 
\frac{\nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)}{\pi_i(\btheta) [1 - \pi_i(\btheta)]} \left[ m_i - \pi_i(\btheta) \right]
\mid \by, \btheta \right]
\nonumber \\
& \sum_{i=1}^p \frac{\nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)}{\pi_i(\btheta) [1 - \pi_i(\btheta)]} 
\left[ p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta) - p(m_i=1 \mid \btheta) \right],
\nonumber
\end{align}
as claimed.
In the particular case where $\pi_i(\btheta)= (1 + e^{\bz_i^{\top} \btheta})^{-1}$, we have
$\nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)= \bz_i  e^{\bz_i^{\top} \btheta} / (1 + e^{\bz_i^{\top} \btheta})^2= \bz_i \pi_i(\btheta) [1 - \pi_i(\btheta)]$,
and then \\
$\nabla_{\btheta}\log p(\by \mid \btheta)= \sum_{i=1}^p \bz_i \left[ p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta) - p(m_i=1 \mid \btheta) \right]$.


\subsection{Proof of Corollary \ref{cor:em_zerograd}}
\label{sec:proof_em_zerograd}

The gradient of 
$f(\btheta)= \sum_{M} p(M \mid \by, \hat{\btheta}^{(k)}) \log p(M \mid \btheta)$
is 
$\nabla_{\btheta} f(\btheta)= \sum_{M} p(M \mid \by, \hat{\btheta}^{(k)}) \nabla_{\btheta} \log p(M \mid \btheta)$.
Using \eqref{eq:grad_priorprob_indep}, we obtain that
\begin{align}
 \nabla_{\btheta} f(\btheta)= \sum_{M} p(M \mid \by, \hat{\btheta}^{(k)})
\sum_{i=1}^p \frac{\nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)}{\pi_i(\btheta) [1 - \pi_i(\btheta)]} \left[ m_i - p(m_i=1 \mid \btheta) \right]
\nonumber \\
= \sum_{i=1}^p \frac{\nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)}{\pi_i(\btheta) [1 - \pi_i(\btheta)]} \left[ p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \hat{\btheta}) - p(m_i=1 \mid \btheta) \right].
\nonumber
\end{align}

In the particular case where $\pi_i(\btheta)= (1 + e^{\bz_i^{\top} \btheta})^{-1}$, from Theorem \ref{thm:ebayes_zerograd} we have that
$[\nabla_{\btheta} \pi_i(\btheta)]/(\pi_i(\btheta) [1 - \pi_i(\btheta)])= \bz_i$
and then 
$\nabla_{\btheta} f(\btheta)= \sum_{i=1}^p \bz_i \left[ p(m_i=1 \mid \by, \btheta) - p(m_i=1 \mid \btheta) \right]= \bZ^{\top} [\hat{\mathbf{r}}(\btheta) - {\bf \pi}(\btheta)]$.
The expression for the hessian $\nabla_{\btheta}^2 f(\btheta)$ follows from straightforward calculus.

\subsection{Proof of Proposition \ref{prop:mstep_fullrank}}
\label{sec:proof_mstep_fullrank}


The goal is to find $\btheta$ such that $\nabla_{\btheta} f(\btheta) + \nabla_{\btheta} \log p(\btheta)= {\bf 0}$.
Plugging in the expression for $\nabla_{\btheta} f(\btheta)$ and $\nabla_{\btheta} \log p(\btheta)= -\frac{1}{g} \bV^{-1} \btheta$,
where $\bV^{-1}= (\bZ^{\top} \bZ)/p$, we seek $\btheta$ such that
\begin{align}
\bZ^{\top} [ \hat{\mathbf{r}} - {\bf \pi}(\btheta) ] - \frac{1}{gp} \bZ^{\top} \bZ \btheta=0
\Leftrightarrow
\bZ^{\top} \left[ {\bf \pi}(\btheta) + \frac{1}{gp} \bZ \btheta \right]= \bZ^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}}, 
\nonumber
\end{align}
where $\pi_i(\btheta)= 1/(1 + e^{- \bz_i^{\top} \btheta})$.
The $q \times q$ matrix $\bU$ contains the unique rows in $\bZ$ and is full-rank, hence $\tilde{\bZ}= \bZ \bU^{-1}$ is a matrix whose unique rows equal the identity matrix. 
Define $\tilde{\btheta}= \bU \btheta$ and consider the problem of finding $\tilde{\btheta}$ such that $\tilde{\bZ}^{\top} [\tilde{{\bf \pi}}(\tilde{\btheta}) - (gp)^{-1} \tilde{\bZ} \tilde{\btheta}]= \tilde{\bZ}^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}}$, where $\tilde{m}_j(\tilde{\btheta})= 1/(1+ e^{- \tilde{z}_j^{\top} \tilde{\btheta}})$. 
we first show how to obtain $\tilde{\btheta}$, and subsequently that one can find $\btheta$ from $\tilde{\btheta}$.

To obtain $\tilde{\btheta}$, intuitively this is simple because the subset of unique rows in $\tilde{\bZ}$ is the identity matrix.
Specifically, the $j^{th}$ equation in $\tilde{\bZ}^{\top} [\tilde{{\bf \pi}}(\tilde{\btheta})  - (gp)^{-1} \tilde{\bZ} \tilde{\btheta} ]= \tilde{\bZ}^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}}$ is 
\begin{align}
 \sum_{i=1}^p  \mbox{I}(\tilde{z}_{ij}=1) \left[ \frac{1}{1 + e^{- \tilde{\bz}_i^{\top} \tilde{\btheta}}} + \frac{\tilde{\bz}_i^{\top} \tilde{\btheta}}{gp} \right]
= \tilde{\bz}_{.j}^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}}
\Rightarrow
\nonumber \\
 \frac{1}{1 + e^{- \theta_j}} + \frac{\tilde{w}_j}{gp} = \frac{\tilde{\bz}_j^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}}}{\sum_{i=1}^p \mbox{I}(\tilde{z}_{ij}=1)}
= \frac{\tilde{\bz}_{.j}^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}}}{\tilde{\bz}_{.j}^{\top} \mathbf{1}},
\nonumber
\end{align}
where $\tilde{\bz}_{.j}$ is the $j^{th}$ column in $\bZ$,
and we used that $\tilde{\bz}_i^{\top} \tilde{\btheta}= \theta_j$, since $\tilde{\bz}_{il}=1$ for $l=j$ and $=0$ otherwise.
We hence have that $\theta_j= h(\tilde{\bz}_{.j}^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}} / \tilde{\bz}_{.j}^{\top} \mathbf{1}, gp)$, 
where $h(a,c)$ denotes the solution (in terms of $w$) to $1/(1+e^{-w}) + w/c= a$.
For the case $c= \infty$, $h(a,\infty)= \log(a/(1-a))$.
More generally, the solution to this univariate equation can be easily found via Newton-Raphson.
%straightforward algebra gives that $\theta_j= \mbox{logit}(\tilde{\bz}_j^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}}/\tilde{\bz}_j^{\top} \mathbf{1})$,

To complete the proof, define $\hat{\btheta}= \bU^{-1} \tilde{\btheta}$,
so that $\tilde{\bZ} \tilde{\btheta}= \bZ \bU^{-1} \bU \hat{\btheta}= \bZ \hat{\btheta}$,
and therefore ${\bf \pi}(\hat{\btheta})= \tilde{{\bf \pi}}(\tilde{\btheta})$.
By definition of $\tilde{\btheta}$ we have that 
\begin{align}
\tilde{\bZ}^{\top} [ \tilde{{\bf \pi}}(\tilde{\btheta}) - \frac{\tilde{\bZ} \tilde{\btheta}}{gp}]= \tilde{\bZ}^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}}
\Rightarrow
(\bU^{-1})^{\top} [ \bZ^{\top} {\bf \pi}(\hat{\btheta})  - \frac{\bZ \hat{\btheta}}{gp} ]= (\bU^{-1})^{\top} \bZ^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}}
\nonumber
\end{align}
which implies that $\bZ^{\top} [{\bf \pi}(\hat{\btheta}) - (gp)^{-1} \bZ \hat{\btheta}] = \bZ^{\top} \hat{\mathbf{r}}$, as claimed.
